Some do not really need some free tickets, they have very high pensions, some even exceptional pensions, but then the view prevailed that it would not be fair for the system to be complicated by 95 percent of the people who need it and who have very low pensions because of a few or a few percent of some who really don’t need it and who can pay these tickets because they don’t use it anyway. I am firmly convinced that someone who has an extraordinary pension today will not use a free ticket because they drive in other ways, some not only with luxury cars, but also with helicopters and airplanes. The system must be – and this was also the lead in 2012, where the same concerns about student and student tickets arose, the system must be as simple as possible and be user friendly as possible. However, he will be affected by those who need him as much as possible. Experience in municipalities where urban transport, free urban transport, has been introduced in recent years, shows that it is the older people who are most affected by these transports. And it is necessary to make these services accessible in the simplest possible way and, of course, in this case in the most friendly way possible, this is, of course, a free ticket from 1 July next year. I think that this is the right direction and the right path especially because the experience of 2012, when we were in a very difficult economic situation, when it was needed to save everywhere, shows that if the resources available are wisely distributed, such a measure can also be taken. Now, however, the circumstances are different, and the reform of this public transport system, which will follow and will follow in the following year and in the coming years, will provide additional savings. So I think this path and this direction is the right one and you have to walk bravely on it. I believe that, maybe not in a year or two, or in five or ten years, Slovenia can do a lot on public transport and actually reach this goal, which some of you have mentioned today as futuristic, where this public transport can actually be free for all permanent users. But you have to follow the steps that make it possible. Now the Ministry is waiting for a big task on this road. Not only is the introduction now, but the system itself will then be significantly more economical, significantly more rational. At present, some lines are too subsidised, some carriers are too subsidised and some are too small. Now about this system is so designed that even where subsidies are not needed, payments are made and on the other hand we pay, as the Minister said, empty seats. They do the same thing to the congregations so that there will be no misunderstanding. For this reason, I appeal that in this reform that will take place in the next two years, the whole picture will be examined and it will be considered that this system that we have today, where we are separating into inter-national passenger transport, which is cared for and subsidized by the State, and the school transport of primary schoolchildren, which is cared for by the municipalities, is uniform. We have today cases where buses drive on routes where they drive at the same time or with a delay of a few minutes a school bus, which drives three students, 50 seats in this bus, and in three minutes a cross-border passenger traffic, which is subsidized by the country, which drives maybe five people, is also all 50 subsidised. For this reason, it is my view that the unification of this system should be considered. This would, on the one hand, relieve the financial municipalities and make them more competitive between individual carriers, since what was mentioned by colleague Lisca is true. In recent years, some have been greatly strengthened. Through this monopoly, they may interfere with this market in such a way as to distort it to the extent that many smaller ones who could provide services in a particular smaller area cannot, in their own words, come along. However, we could provide lower prices and also quality services. That's why I think it's my job to make this system smarter. Now I don't know if it's fuller. I hope it's after a year. But I think that the first step that should be appropriate is obviously not to say how these new concessions will be awarded today. I don't think there's much of a philosophy here. Where cross-border lines are concerned, cross-border lines will be tendered, but where areas are concerned, calls must be made within the areas. It is necessary to allow as many different carriers as possible to provide these services. In the debate, it was said that a line transport subsidised by the State was established that the interest of the State was that buses travelling between places were complete and that was also one of the reasons why all pensioners, including those with very good pensions, could be provided free of charge. It is therefore right to allow such transport for war veterans as well. In my debate, I touched a particular group at risk. These are veteran war veterans who receive a veteran supplement. In view of the fact that this free transport measure for pensioners, disabled persons and athletes is not a social measure during the debate, we can also correct this measure in order to allow it for all veterans. We will propose an amendment in this direction. At this stage, I am inviting all the parliamentary groups to participate in the drafting of these amendments. Last but not least, we are in the time before the day of sovereignty, which will be 25. October, and there may also be one gesture to worship all those who have received the status of war veteran in accordance with the law. Thank you.
